## Session: Mar 28, 2026 - 21:00 UTC
**Status:** Added 3 new tools (244-246), 727 lines this session

### New Features Added:

1. ✅ **TOOL 244 — Concrete Thermal Mass Energy Credit Calculator (🏢)** — ACI 122R / ASHRAE 90.1-2019 §5.5.3.1 / LEED v4.1 EAc2. Quantifies energy savings from concrete thermal mass in buildings. 6 ASHRAE climate zones with thermal mass credit factors (El Paso CZ 2B hot-dry has highest benefit at 12% — large diurnal swing). 10 wall assembly presets: 8"/12" CMU grouted, 6"/8"/10" CIP walls, 5.5"/7.25"/9.25" tilt-up panels, 6"/8" precast — each with mass (lb/SF), heat capacity (Btu/SF·°F), and R-value. 7 floor assembly presets: 4"/5"/6"/8" SOG, 5"/7" elevated deck, 8" hollow-core plank. Building inputs: conditioned SF, baseline EUI (kBtu/SF/yr), energy cost ($/kBtu). Physics engine: sums wall/floor mass and heat capacity, computes average HC per conditioned SF, checks ASHRAE 90.1 §5.5.3.1 mass wall threshold (HC ≥ 7.0 Btu/ft²·°F), calculates annual energy savings and cost savings. ASHRAE mass wall qualification badge (relaxed U-factor requirements when qualified). LEED v4.1 EAc2 point estimate. Reference table with ASHRAE 90.1-2019 §5.5.3.1, Table 5.5-3, ACI 122R-14 §4.2, IECC 2021 provisions. Print formal ACI 122R/ASHRAE 90.1 energy credit analysis with EOR/Consultant sign-off. Business case: El Paso (CZ 2B) has highest thermal mass benefit in the US — 30-40°F diurnal temperature swings mean concrete walls absorb daytime heat and release at night, reducing both cooling and heating loads 8-15%. A 50,000 SF tilt-up warehouse with 8" panels saves $4,000-$8,000/year vs metal building.

2. ✅ **TOOL 245 — Concrete Pump Sizing & Selection Guide (🚛)** — ACI 304.2R / ASME B30.27 / Schwing/Putzmeister specs. Match pour requirements to pump capacity with cost optimization. 9 pump type presets with El Paso 2025 daily rates: trailer/line pump ($850), 32m boom ($1,800), 36m ($2,100), 42m ($2,400), 47m ($2,800), 52m ($3,200), 58m+ ($3,800), placing boom ($650), stationary high-rise ($1,200) — each with min/max rate (yd³/hr), max reach (ft), line size, slump requirement, prime/waste yd³. Pour inputs: total yd³, target duration (hrs), max reach needed, design slump, element type (9 options), site access (4 options), max aggregate size, cold joint window. Analysis engine: calculates required rate, adds 25% buffer, checks cold joint safe rate (truckYd × 60 / CJ window), finds suitable pumps by capacity and reach, sorts by daily cost. Cold joint risk warning when required rate exceeds safe rate. Slump pumpability guide (ACI 304.2R): slump vs pumpability rating and notes. Aggregate size warning (max ≤ 1/3 line diameter). Recommends lowest-cost adequate pump with alternatives listed. Print ACI 304.2R/ASME B30.27 formal selection analysis. Business case: wrong pump selection costs both ways — undersized = slow rate = cold joint risk + overtime; oversized = $1,000-2,000/day extra for unused capacity. A 150 yd³ elevated deck at 100 ft reach needs 36-42m boom at $2,100-2,400 — not 52m at $3,200.

3. ✅ **TOOL 246 — Concrete Contractor Insurance Coverage Analyzer (🛡️)** — ISO CGL / AIA A201 §11 / TX DOI requirements. Verify your coverage meets project bid requirements before submission. 8 project type presets with typical limits: Private Commercial <$500k through Federal/Miller Act — each with CGL per-occ/aggregate, auto CSL, umbrella, WC requirement, bond requirement. Your coverage inputs: CGL occ/agg, auto, umbrella, WC/EL, deductible, annual premium, EMR. 9 common CGL exclusion checkboxes (EIFS, subsidence, residential, condo, silica, mold, etc.) with risk ratings (HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW). Analysis engine: compares your limits to project requirements, identifies gaps with $ shortfall, checks EMR (>1.20 = disqualified from most GC preQual, <0.85 = excellent), flags policy exclusions with risk assessment. Coverage adequacy banner (green = adequate, red = gaps detected). Bond requirement notice. Premium as % of contract value. AIA A201 §11 reference table with all project types. Print formal ISO CGL/AIA A201 coverage adequacy analysis with status summary and sign-off. Business case: bidding without verifying insurance meets contract requirements = award rejection or last-minute scramble. Public projects (ISD, municipal, TxDOT) have statutory minimums that cannot be waived. $2M umbrella costs ~$3,000-5,000/year but opens access to $500k-$2M project bids. EMR >1.20 disqualifies from GC bid lists entirely.

Physics engine: flat slab volume (total thickness × SF) + rib fill volume (trapezoidal prism per SDI C-2017 — avg rib width × rib height × ribs/LF × total SF). Shows rib fill premium % — 1.5" Type B deck adds ~14% extra volume vs flat-slab calculation; 3" W3 adds ~22%. Dead load psf calculation (weight lbs and tons). Cost with OH+profit markup. Rib fill premium alert when >15% (flags exact $ under-ordered if estimated as flat slab). Save named estimates to log. Print AISC/SDI formal estimate with EOR/GC sign-off and ACI 318-19 §26.3 cover note. Business case: most concrete estimators price composite deck as flat slab (thickness × SF ÷ 27) — this consistently under-orders by 12–25% depending on deck profile, which means paying for an emergency re-order mid-pour or running short.

2. ✅ **TOOL 236 — Bid Quote Expiry Monitor (⏰)** — ConsensusDocs §3.4 / ACI 301-16. Track all sub and material quotes for a bid and flag expired or expiring items before submission. Per quote: vendor/sub company, scope (17 presets: ready-mix, rebar fab, pump, forming, saw cutting, PT, testing, fiber, etc.), quote amount, quote date, expiry date, contact/rep, required vs optional vs budget-only flag, notes/exclusions. Live expiry countdown per quote: EXPIRED (red ⛔ DO NOT SUBMIT), ≤3d (amber ⚠️), ≤7d (yellow), OK/current (green). Dashboard: total, expired, urgent ≤3d, current counts. Red DO NOT SUBMIT banner when any required quotes expired. Required-only total vs grand total. Quote register sorted by urgency. Clear all for new bid. Print formal quote expiry register with expired-quote stop block and policy statement. Business case: submitting a bid with expired pump or rebar pricing = locked-in exposure if awarded. Concrete prices can change 5–10% in 2 weeks; expired quotes = no binding sub pricing = change order risk from day one.

3. ✅ **TOOL 237 — Concrete Pump Blockage Incident Log (🚨)** — ACI 304.2R §6.4 / OSHA 1926.1412 / 1926.306. Contemporaneous pump line blockage documentation and safety compliance record. Built-in ACI 304.2R §6.4 9-step blockage response protocol prominently displayed: relieve pressure to 0 psi, establish 10-ft exclusion zone, full PPE, no disconnecting pressurized couplings, rubber mallet only, re-prime before resuming. Per incident: project, date/time, pump type (5 options), operator/company, blockage cause (11 presets: low slump, line set during pause, foreign material, inadequate prime, elbow blockage, hose kink, aggregate plug, air lock, high rate vs slump, reverse pressure, unknown), location in line, downtime minutes, pour rate (yd³/hr for lost-production calc), cold joint outcome (4 options), injury/near-miss status (5 options including OSHA recordable), resolution/corrective action, status (5 options). Auto-computes lost yd³ at entered pour rate. Open incident alert banner (⛔ unresolved = exclusion zone active). Dashboard: total incidents, open count, total downtime, estimated lost revenue. Print ACI 304.2R/OSHA 1926.1412 formal log: open incidents highlighted, cold joint events flagged, safety protocol reference, Pump Operator/Foreman/GC Superintendent triple sign-off. Business case: (1) Safety — pressurized pump line disconnect has caused operator fatalities; ACI 304.2R §6.4 requires full pressure relief before hose work. (2) Commercial — 45 min blockage × $280/hr pump rate + 22 yd³ lost production at $180/yd³ installed = $4,900 in documented downtime the pump company can credit and the GC can't dispute without a log.

ACI 318-19 Table 19.3.3.1 reference card (11 exposure classes with max w/c and min f'c: F0/S0 → no limit, F1 0.45/4500 psi, F2/F3 0.40/4500, S2 0.45/4500, S3 0.45/4500, C1/C2 0.40/5000, W1 0.50/3500, W2 0.45/4000). Each 1 gal/yd³ added to a 10 yd³ load at 564 lb/yd³ cement raises w/c by 0.016 — enough to push a 0.44 mix to 0.46 (over F1 limit). Without a contemporaneous calculation and log, the contractor has no record to defend against a 28-day cylinder failure that was caused by unauthorized water addition.

Inputs: wall LF, height, panel width/height (in), tie type (snap/coil rod/she-bolt/custom), tie capacity, tie spacing H&V, form panel cost $/SF, tie cost $/ea, form release $/SF, install labor $/SF, waler system option (none/2x6/steel W-beam), OH%, profit%. Physics: contact SF = LF×ht×2 faces; panel count from panel SF; tie rows×cols from spacing grid; design pressure per ACI 347R hydrostatic model (min 600 psf, max 2,000 psf); tie load = pressure × tributary area; utilization % vs tie capacity. Verdicts: ✅ SAFE (≤75%), ⚠️ OK–Near Limit (75-100%), ⛔ EXCEEDS CAPACITY with exact remedy (reduce spacing or upgrade tie). Full cost breakdown: panel material + ties + release agent + walers + install labor + OH+profit = total. Unit rates: $/LF and $/SF contact. Save up to 20 estimates. Print ACI 347R-14 formal estimate with utilization table, OSHA 1926.703 disclaimer, Estimator/Form Foreman/GC Inspector triple sign-off. ACI 347R panel type quick reference (plywood 600-1,000 psf, steel-framed 2,000+ psf, snap tie 2,500#, she-bolt 8,000#). Why: Wall form hardware is one of the most consistently under-bid line items — a 200 LF × 12 ft wall at 24" OC snap ties has ~300 ties × $0.38 + release + labor = $8,500+ in scope nobody broke out systematically. The tie utilization check also enforces ACI 347R before pour.

2. ✅ **TOOL 215 — Concrete Allowable Crack Width Checker (🔍)** — ACI 224R-01 Table 4.1 / ACI 318-19 §24.3. Built-in ACI 224R Table 4.1 reference: 7 exposure conditions with allowable widths in inches and mil (dry air 16 mil → water-retaining 4 mil). Crack log: project, location/grid, exposure condition, measured width (mil), crack length, crack type (10 presets: plastic shrinkage/drying/settlement/structural flexure/shear/thermal/cold joint/corrosion/map crazing/unknown), inspector initials, date, notes. Auto-verdict: PASS (≤85% limit), MARGINAL (85-100%), FAIL (>limit). Structural crack types (flexural/shear) auto-flag EOR evaluation requirement regardless of measured width. Dashboard: total cracks, fail count (red banner), EOR-required count. Red ⛔ EOR banner when any FAIL entries per ACI 318-19 §26.12.4. Print ACI 224R-01 formal log: full table with measured vs limit columns, FAIL rows highlighted red, structural stop block, QC Inspector/Project Engineer/EOR triple sign-off, 5-year retention note. Delete per entry. Global localStorage. El Paso: drying shrinkage in arid climate routinely produces 8-14 mil cracks on unsealed warehouse slabs — this tool tells you exactly whether the 12 mil crack on a moist-air-exposure floor is within ACI tolerance (12 mil limit) or a violation. Creates the contemporaneous ACI-cited record that prevents GC rejection of accepted crack widths.

3. ✅ **TOOL 216 — Contractor Pre-Qualification Score Card (⭐)** — AIA A401 / ConsensusDocs 750 — 100-point weighted evaluation system for concrete subs and suppliers. 10 scored criteria (10 pts each): Experience & years in business, Project references (3+ verifiable), Safety/EMR, Insurance currency (CGL/WC/Auto), Bonding capacity, Crew capacity & owned equipment, Financial stability, Communication & responsiveness, Quality of past workmanship, Price competitiveness. Per criterion: 4-5 calibrated scoring options shown as scoring guide text. Total score: 0–100. Automatic verdict: ✅ APPROVED (≥80 = standard retainage, no extra bond) / ⚠️ CONDITIONAL (60-79 = 10% retainage, milestone payments, bond recommended >$50k) / 🔶 HIGH RISK (40-59 = 15% retainage, P&P bond required) / ⛔ DO NOT AWARD (<40). Per-criterion color-coded score grid in results. Ranked register: all evaluated companies sorted by score with rank, scope, score, verdict, evaluator. Print formal register: ranked table, verdict guide, AIA A401 / ConsensusDocs 750 reference, PM/Principal dual sign-off. Per-company delete. Global localStorage. Business case: most concrete contractors make sub selection decisions on gut feel and price — this creates the documented 100-point basis for awarding scope that surety underwriters and GC preQual audits look for. After 10+ evaluations, the ranked register shows "this pump company scored 82 but the other scored 47" and supports defensible award decisions.

Computes: panel weight (kips), wind force, brace axial load, utilization %, min braces per panel, total braces all panels, brace attach height (2/3 H per TCA), brace length, floor horizontal thrust. Verdicts: ✅ PASS, ⚠️ OK-Near Limit (≥80% util), ⛔ EXCEEDS with exact max safe spacing or min capacity required. Save calcs to history. Print TCA/OSHA formal record with Erection Foreman/Structural Engineer/GC Superintendent triple sign-off. TCA quick reference table (min 2 braces/panel, max 60° brace angle, min 3,000 PSI before erection, 4" floor slab min for brace embed). Tilt-up erection bracing is the highest single-event collapse risk on commercial construction — form blowouts and brace failures routinely occur when brace load is estimated by feel. A 24'×20' panel at 7" thick at 20 psf wind needs 12,000 lb capacity minimum at 60°; upgrading to double braces at 8' spacing requires only 8,000 lb each. This tool quantifies it before the crane picks the first panel.

2. ✅ **TOOL 191 — Concrete Surface Sealer & Hardener Estimator (🛡️)** — ACI 302.1R-15 / ASTM C1315 / ASTM C309 / ACI 308R surface treatment quantity and cost calculator. 8 product presets with coverage rates and El Paso 2025 pricing: Cure & Seal 30% solids (200 SF/gal, $0.18/SF), Cure & Seal 40% solids (150 SF/gal, $0.22/SF), Sodium Silicate Densifier ASTM C1315 (300 SF/gal, $0.12/SF), Lithium Silicate Densifier (250 SF/gal, $0.20/SF), Penetrating Silane/Siloxane DOT-grade (150 SF/gal, $0.35/SF), Epoxy Sealer 100% solids ASTM C881 (160 SF/gal, $0.65/SF), Polyurethane 2-coat (300 SF/gal, $0.45/SF), Acrylic Dissipating (200 SF/gal, $0.14/SF). Inputs: area SF, coats, product, coverage SF/gal (auto-fills), $/SF material, labor $/SF, OH%, profit%, waste%. Computes: net gallons (SF×coats÷coverage), order gallons (+waste%), 5-gal pail count, material/labor/markup breakdown, total bid, $/SF all-in. Auto-fills coverage and price on product selection. Save estimates to history. Print ACI 302.1R-15/ASTM C309/C1315 formal estimate: line items, pail count, ACI 302.1R warning (never densify with sodium silicate over colored/integral-pigment concrete; never apply membrane C309 before coating — use dissipating type). Sealer scope is consistently either missed (no curing compound in bid) or the wrong product specified — this makes the right product selection a 10-second task and prices it from actual gal/SF quantities instead of a lump sum guess.

3. ✅ **TOOL 192 — Subgrade Proof Roll Observation Log (🚜)** — TxDOT Item 216 / ACI 360R-10 §5.2 / ASTM D6951 contemporaneous proof roll observation record. Per-zone entry: project, zone/grid ref, date, roll equipment (4 options: loaded dump truck 25t, tandem axle 18t, vibratory roller 10t, pneumatic roller), soil type (7 options incl. El Paso caliche and expansive clay), moisture condition (5 options), rutting observed (None/<0.5"/0.5"–1"/1"–2"/>2"), pumping/heave severity (None/Slight/Moderate/Severe), observed-by initials, notes/remediation plan. Auto-disposition engine: pumping≥2 or rut≥2" → FAIL — Engineer Required; rut or pump observed → Hold — Re-compact; otherwise → Approved. ⛔ DO NOT PLACE CONCRETE red banner listing all unresolved zones; ✅ CLEARED banner when all zones approved. Log with color-coded disposition (green/amber/red). Print TxDOT Item 216/ACI 360R-10 §5.2 formal log: fail rows highlighted red, hold rows amber, DO NOT PLACE notice, Inspector/Foreman/Geotechnical Engineer/GC Superintendent 4-way sign-off, 5-year retention note. TCA/ACI reference table (5 observation scenarios with action/disposition). El Paso notes (caliche typically approves first roll; expansive south EP clay requires lime stabilization before roll). Without a contemporaneous proof roll record, GCs can dispute soft-spot conditions post-pour and claim you placed concrete on unapproved subgrade — a $30,000–$80,000 slab replacement liability that this tool prevents.

Archive completed inspections. Print ACI 304.2R/OSHA 1926.1412 formal checklist: category-grouped items, status column, fail rows highlighted, triple Pump Operator/Foreman/GC Superintendent sign-off. Most pump failures occur from skipped pre-shift inspections — outrigger collapse, boom failure, and discharge hose blowouts are all preventable with documented checks.

3. ✅ **TOOL 171 — Excusable Delay Days Calculator & Time Extension Exhibit (☁️)** — AIA A201 §8.3 / ConsensusDocs §6.3.1 — Formal delay day tracking and time extension exhibit generator. 9 delay type presets across 2 categories: Weather/Force Majeure (rain >0.5", extreme heat >105°F, hard freeze <28°F, dust storm) and Owner/GC-Caused (owner-directed suspension, differing site condition, GC materials late, RFI hold). Each preset includes contract basis reference (AIA §8.3.1, §14.3, FAR 52.236-2, etc.). Per-event logging: start date, days lost (supports 0.5-day increments), notice sent status (4 options including late notice warning), supporting evidence field (NOAA station, GC letter, RFI number). Dashboard: total excusable days, weather/FM days, owner/GC-caused days, events logged. Daily cost impact calculator when overhead rate entered ($/day × delay days). Notice compliance warnings (AIA requires 21-day notice, ConsensusDocs 10-15 days). Print formal Time Extension Exhibit: project info, delay log table with contract basis, totals with cost impact, summary statement, legal language, triple Contractor/GC/Owner sign-off. Without contemporaneous delay documentation with written notice, time extension claims are waived — this forces the habit of logging every event as it occurs.

4. ✅ **TOOL 172 — Concrete Surface Profile (CSP) Log (🔲)** — ICRI 310.2R / SSPC-SP 13 — Floor surface profile documentation for coating and overlay systems. Full ICRI CSP 1-10 reference chart with prep method, profile depth, and typical uses per level. 9 prep method presets (acid etch through heavy scarify). 12 coating system presets (epoxy thin-film, self-leveling, polyurethane, MMA, urethane cement, etc.). Per-test: project, area/grid, SF, prep method used, coating system planned, manufacturer-required CSP, achieved CSP (chip comparison). Auto-verdict: PASS if achieved CSP meets or exceeds required; FAIL if insufficient profile. Dashboard: areas tested, pass, fail counts. Red ⛔ DO NOT APPLY COATING banner when any fails on record. Print ICRI 310.2R formal CSP log: full test table, pass/fail color-coded, SSPC-SP 13 reference, triple QC/Coating Contractor/Owner sign-off. Coating applied to incorrect CSP = delamination, bond failure, and voided manufacturer warranty — this creates the QC record that proves profile was verified.

5. ✅ **TOOL 173 — Concrete Truck Turnaround Time Log (🚚)** — ASTM C94 / ACI 304R — Per-truck time tracking for ready-mix deliveries. Per-load: ticket #, truck ID, load yd³, batch time (plant), arrival time, start discharge, end discharge, depart site. Auto-computes: transit time (batch→arrive), site time (arrive→depart), total turnaround, batch-to-end-discharge time. ASTM C94 §11.6 violation check: if batch-to-end-discharge exceeds 90 minutes, auto-flags VIOLATION (non-conforming concrete). Dashboard: trucks logged, total yd³, avg turnaround, slow trucks (>30 min), ASTM violations. Red ⛔ banner when violations exist with ACI 318-19 §26.4.3 notification requirement. Print ASTM C94 formal log: full time table, violation rows highlighted, transit/site/turn columns, ASTM §11.6/§11.7 (retarder extension) notes, triple QC/Foreman/Plant Rep sign-off. Tracks both ASTM compliance AND plant billing disputes (plant charges based on site time). Cold joint risk correlates with slow turnaround — if trucks are taking 35+ minutes each, placement rate is too slow.

6. ✅ **TOOL 174 — Concrete Batch Plant Calibration Verification Log (🏭)** — ASTM C94 §11 / NRMCA QC3 — Verify plant scale and meter calibration before accepting concrete. 10 scale/meter type presets (cement scale, water meter, coarse/fine agg, admixture dispensers, ice scale, fly ash, slag, silica fume). Each with ASTM C94 tolerance (cement/water ±1%, aggregates ±2%, admixtures ±3%). Per-check: plant name, verification date, scale type, target test weight (lbs), actual reading, last calibration date, cert reference, technician. Auto-computes: variance %, tolerance lookup, PASS/FAIL verdict. Dashboard: checks logged, pass, fail counts. Red ⛔ CALIBRATION FAILURES banner when out-of-tolerance scales on record with ASTM C94 §11.3 recalibration requirement. ASTM C94 tolerance reference table in tool. Print NRMCA QC3 formal calibration verification log: full table with variance %, tolerance, verdict columns, fail rows highlighted, NRMCA 6-month calibration frequency note, triple QC/Plant QC Manager/GC Materials Engineer sign-off. Out-of-tolerance scales produce non-conforming concrete — most contractors assume plant calibration is current without verification.

Companion to Tool 18 (PT Stressing Log). Reference tables: duct type μ/K coefficients (unbonded mono μ=0.05, grouted metal μ=0.20, grouted HDPE μ=0.07) and strand Aps/Eps (½"/0.6" 270k). Per tendon: span, CGS sag/drape, jacking force, Aps, Eps, duct type, profile type (parabolic/banded/flat), seating loss. Physics: angular change α = 8×sag/L × profile factor; friction loss e^(μα+KL); average effective force Pavg; theoretical elongation Δ = Pavg×L×12/(Aps×Eps) − seating loss. Compare to measured field elongation: variance % computation with PTI ±7% tolerance — PASS or FAIL verdict. FAIL triggers red engineering stop banner: "Do NOT grout until PT EOR resolves per ACI 318-19 §26.10.3." Save up to 50 tendon records. Print PTI/ACI formal log with FAIL rows highlighted red and PT Engineer/Stressing Contractor/GC triple sign-off. PT elongation discrepancies are the most common PT dispute — structural severity ranges from over-stressed tendons to complete strand failure. Having the theoretical calculation documented before stressing gives the field team a target value to verify against.

Steps: Technical Review, Scope Verification (Scope Gap Analyzer complete), Pricing Confirmation (quotes locked), Risk Matrix Review (contingency adequate), T&C / Contract Review (bonding requirements), Insurance Verified (CGL/WC/Auto current), Bond Capacity Confirmed (P&P available if needed), Sub Bid Coverage (pump/rebar/forming confirmed), Escalation Check (quotes <7 days, clause applied), Final Number Sign-Off (principal approved), Proposal Formatted (T&C/exclusions attached), Submitted to GC (confirmation saved). Per-step status cycle: Open → In Progress → Approved → Waived. Initials + notes field per step. Progress bar (% complete). ⚠️ DO NOT SUBMIT banner listing open steps while incomplete. ✅ APPROVED TO SUBMIT green banner at 100%. Mark All Approved / Reset All buttons. Print formal Approval Sheet: step table with status color-coding, ACI/ConsensusDocs reference notes, Estimator/PM/Principal triple sign-off. Per-session localStorage. Why: most scope disputes, pricing errors, and insurance gaps are discovered AFTER the bid is submitted — when they're expensive to fix. This forces the pre-submission review that prevents them.

2. ✅ **TOOL 140 — Site Mobilization Cost Estimator (🚛)** — Full mob/demob cost builder for concrete field work. ACI 347R-14 / OSHA 1926.20 / ConsensusDocs §3. 23 pre-loaded line items across 5 categories: Personnel Travel (foreman drive time $42/hr, crew carpool $85/trip, per diem $75/day), Equipment Mob (boom pump mob $850/ls, line pump $450/ls, laser screed $280/ls, telehandler $350/ls, generator $150/ls, pickup truck fuel $45/day), Temp Facilities (portable toilet $165/mo, job trailer $225/mo, temp fencing $2.50/LF, temp power $350/ls), Setup Labor (survey/layout $42/hr, grade check $35/hr, form staging $28/hr, demob/strip $28/hr, punch day $350/day), Admin / Other (permits, traffic control, safety supplies $125/job, first aid $65/job, misc consumables $85/ls). Check/uncheck to include. Editable qty/rate per item. Add custom items. OH% + profit% markup. Summary: subtotal/OH/profit/total + $/yd³ adder when yd³ entered (quantifies remote job premium). Reset to Defaults. Print ACI/OSHA/ConsensusDocs formal estimate: included items only, all columns, mob adder, exclusions note, triple sign-off. El Paso to Midland (300 mi) = significant mob burden on small pours. Most concrete bids have no explicit mob line item — it gets absorbed into unit rates inconsistently, meaning out-of-town jobs are routinely underpriced.

3. ✅ **TOOL 141 — Concrete Form Blowout Risk Calculator (💥)** — ACI 347R-14 lateral pressure vs tie design check. Physics: ACI 347R Eq. 2 (walls): p = CwCc[150 + 9000R/T] ≤ w·H, min 600 psf; Eq. 4 (columns): p = CwCc[150 + 43400/T + 2800R/T] ≤ w·H. Cw = unit weight factor (auto-computed from pcf input). Inputs: element type (wall/column), wall height (ft), pour rate (ft/hr), concrete temp (°F), unit weight (pcf), tie type (7 presets: snap tie 1,750# $0.38 → coil rod 3/4" 12,000# $8.50, + custom), tie capacity (auto-fills from preset), proposed spacing (inches OC). Computes: design pressure (psf), hydrostatic max, Cw factor, tie load demand (pressure × spacing²), tie utilization % vs ACI 347R target (≤75% = safe). Verdicts: ✅ SAFE (≤75%), ⚠️ WARNING (75–100%), ⛔ BLOWOUT RISK (>100%). BLOWOUT output: exact max safe spacing in inches OR minimum required tie capacity, OR required pour rate reduction. Save checks to history (25 max). Print ACI 347R-14 formal calc sheet: full parameters table, design pressure derivation, tie check table, verdict, OSHA 1926.703 disclaimer (formwork must be inspected by PE), EOR/Foreman/Inspector triple sign-off. El Paso: 12-ft walls at 4 ft/hr pour rate with 50°F winter concrete temp = dramatically higher pressure than summer pour at 85°F — the same tie layout can be safe in summer and BLOWOUT RISK in winter. Without this check, form decisions are made by feel. Form blowout = structural collapse, injury, catastrophic concrete loss, and project delay.

1. ✅ **TOOL 133 — Loading Dock Pit & Approach Apron Estimator (🏗️)** — ACI 318-19 / ACI 360R-10 / MHI MH30.1 dock pit concrete estimator. Inputs: pit count, pit L×W×depth, pit wall thickness, pit floor thickness, approach apron SF/dock and thickness, dock leveler embed type (none/weld plate $450/full frame $650). El Paso 2025 pricing: concrete $/yd³, forms $/SF, rebar $/lb, labor $/hr, OH%, profit%. Physics: separate wall volume (4 walls, 2 long + 2 short), pit floor volume, apron volume — each computed precisely. Formwork: pit wall contact SF both sides plus apron edge form. Rebar: 95 lbs/yd³ for pit (congested walls), 75 lbs/yd³ for apron. Labor: 1.2 hrs/yd³ all-in. Result: total yd³, pit yd³ vs apron yd³, formwork SF, rebar lbs/tons, itemized cost table, total bid, $/dock, $/yd³. Save to history (20 max). Print ACI 318-19/MHI MH30.1 formal estimate with exclusions block (excavation, dewatering, leveler equipment, bumpers, framing, permits) and Estimator/PM/Client triple sign-off. ACI quick reference table (pit floor 6" min/4ksi, pit walls 8" min/4ksi, apron 8" min/4.5ksi, leveler frame embed notes). Dock pit concrete is routinely missed or treated as a single line item in warehouse bids — a typical 8-dock distribution center has ~480 SF of pit floor, ~640 SF of pit walls, 2,400 SF of approach apron, and 8 leveler embeds that together add $65,000–$90,000+ to the scope.

2. ✅ **TOOL 134 — Slab Bearing Capacity Check (Rack/Equipment) (⚖️)** — ACI 318-19 §22.6 / ACI 360R-10 §6.3 bearing stress + punching shear verification for concentrated rack leg or equipment loads on slab-on-grade. Inputs: description/tag, applied load per leg (lbs), load factor (1.0–1.6 per load type: dead only/dead+live/forklift/seismic), base plate L×W (in), slab thickness (in), f'c PSI (3,000–6,000), subgrade k-value (50–300 pci), reinforcement class. Two structural checks: (1) Bearing Stress per ACI 318-19 §22.6.1 — actual stress = factored load ÷ plate area vs allowable = 0.85 × φ × f'c (φ=0.65); (2) Punching Shear per ACI 318-19 §22.6.5 — Vc = 4 × φ × √f'c × bo × d at d/2 from plate edge. Radius of relative stiffness computed (Westergaard). Verdicts per check: ✓ OK or ✗ EXCEEDS. Overall: PASS / WARNING (>80% ratio) / FAIL. FAIL output: exact plate size needed or slab thickness required to resolve. Save checks to history. Print ACI 318-19/360R-10 formal calc sheet with parameters table, results table, EOR sign-off. Warehouse rack selection is one of the most common scope gaps on concrete bids — a typical narrow-aisle rack at 12,000 lbs/leg is fine on a 6" slab; a pushback rack at 25,000 lbs/leg is not.

3. ✅ **TOOL 135 — Prevailing Wage Certified Payroll Summary / WH-347 (📜)** — DOL WH-347 / Davis-Bacon & Related Acts / 29 CFR §5.5(a)(4) weekly certified payroll generator. 14 classification presets with El Paso 2025 TX prevailing wage rates and fringe benefit rates (Concrete Finisher $28.75+$8.50 fringes, Carpenter $31.50+$9.75, Cement Mason $29.25+$8.75, Laborer $21.50+$6.25, Operating Engineer $36.75–$38.50+$13–13.50, Ironworker $34.25–$36.00+$11.25–12, Foreman $35+$10.50, Truck Driver $26.50+$7.50, etc.). Project header: project/prime/payroll number/week ending/project type (TxDOT/Federal/ISD/Municipal)/contract value. Per-employee entry: name, classification (auto-fills prevailing wage + fringes), prevailing $/hr, actual $/hr, regular hours, OT hours, fringe benefit $/hr, other deductions $, SSN last 4. Auto-computes: gross wages (OT at 1.5×), FICA (7.65%), net pay per employee. Real-time violation detection: actual rate below prevailing wage → red VIOLATION badge + warning banner (DOL debarment risk). Totals: employees, total hours, total gross, total net, total fringes. Print DOL WH-347 format: full payroll table with violation rows highlighted, certification statement (29 CFR §5.5(a)(1)), penalty notice (18 U.S.C. §1001 — up to $1,000 + 1 year imprisonment for falsification), 3-year retention requirement, triple signature block. Prevailing wage reference table (all 14 classifications). Session-based employee list (cleared on close). Required for TxDOT, federal, and TX Little Miller Act (ISD, municipal) projects — most concrete subs doing public work have no systematic WH-347 generator.

  - CONCRETE PLACEMENT RATE OPTIMIZER (🏎️): ACI 304R/347R placement rate planning tool. 12 element type presets with typical placement rates (SOG ≤6" 30 yd³/hr, Wall >20' 8 yd³/hr, Elevated Deck 15 yd³/hr, Drilled Shaft 10 yd³/hr, etc.), recommended crew sizes, and finishing requirements. Inputs: element type, total yd³, crew size, target rate, cold joint window (45/60/90/120 min), truck capacity, pour start time. Computes: pour duration, start-to-finish time window, truck count, order yd³ (3% waste), truck delivery interval. COLD JOINT RISK analysis: compares truck interval to CJ window — green ✓ SAFE or red ⚠️ RISK with recommended minimum rate and alternate truck count. Short-load warning. Crew relief alert for >8hr pours. Pump capacity check for high rates. Save analyses to history. ACI 304R placement rate reference table. El Paso summer CJ window as low as 30-45 min without retarder — this quantifies the risk before pour day.
  - CONCRETE VAPOR EMISSION TEST LOG (💨): ASTM F1869/F2170/F2420/F710 moisture vapor emission testing and tracking. 4 test method presets (CaCl₂, RH probe, humidity hood, plastic sheet) with units and procedures. 9 flooring type presets with manufacturer moisture limits (epoxy 3 lbs/75% RH, VCT 5 lbs/80%, polished concrete 8 lbs/90%, etc.). Per test: project/element, location/grid, date, slab age, method, result value, planned flooring, tested-by, notes. Auto-verdict: PASS/MARGINAL/FAIL against flooring manufacturer limits. Dashboard: pass/marginal/fail counts. Red ⛔ DO NOT INSTALL banner when FAIL results exist with ASTM F3010 vapor mitigation requirement. Print ASTM F1869/F2170 formal log: full table with pass/fail color-coded rows, failed-zone alert block, F710 testing protocol notes, QC/PM/Flooring Contractor triple sign-off. Flooring limit reference table. Moisture-related flooring failures are the #1 warranty claim on commercial buildings — this creates the pre-installation contemporaneous record.
  - EMBEDDED ITEM CONFLICT CHECKER (🔍): ACI 318-19 §26.6 / ACI 117-10 coordination and clash documentation. 16 embedded item type presets (conduit, rebar, PT strand, anchor bolts, embed plates, sleeves, radiant tubing, waterstop, etc.). Per conflict: project/element, item 1 vs item 2, location/grid, severity (Low/Medium/High/Critical), description, proposed resolution, reported-by. Status cycle: Open → RFI Submitted → Under Review → Resolved → Closed. Dashboard: total, open, critical open (red alert). ⛔ DO NOT POUR banner when critical conflicts open — requires EOR resolution. Auto-numbered EC-001+. Print ACI 318-19/117-10 formal conflict log: severity-coded rows, open-conflict alert block, position tolerance references (±1" horizontal, ±1/4" elevation), 4-way sign-off. Conflicts discovered after pour = core drilling ($125-500/ea) + structural review ($2,500+). This catches them before concrete.

Per-notice: project, delay type, date occurred, notice date, days impacted (est.), impact type (schedule extension / cost / both / TBD), party who directed, notice method (email/certified mail/hand delivery/RFI), factual description, supporting docs referenced. Status cycle: Open → Submitted to GC → Under Review → Time Extension Granted → Cost Claim Pending → Resolved → Withdrawn. Dashboard: total notices, open count, total days claimed, cost impact notices. Red alert banner when open claims exist (AIA A201 §15.1 escalation reminder). Print formal Delay Claim Notice Letter: AIA A201 §8.3 / ConsensusDocs §6.3.1 citation, nature of delay table, description, schedule/cost impact table, reservation of rights paragraph (time extension + equitable adjustment + preserved rights), request for written acknowledgment within 7 days, triple Contractor/GC/Owner sign-off. Global localStorage. Why: AIA A201 §8.3.1 requires written notice within 21 days of first delay occurrence — missing this window = waived time extension rights AND waived cost claims. Every concrete sub who has worked on commercial projects has lost a delay claim because it wasn't in writing within the contract window.
  - TILT-UP PANEL POUR BED PLANNER (🏗️): TCA Design Manual / ACI 551.1R-14 — complete panel schedule and pour bed setup. Add panel types by mark with W × H (ft), thickness (in), qty, PSI (3500/4000/5000), erection sequence #, opening cutout count, notes/embeds. Auto-computes: panel SF, yd³/panel (with 3% waste), panel weight (lbs at 150 pcf) per type. Dashboard: panel types, total SF, total panel yd³, approximate combined weight. Bond Breaker & Pour Bed Material section: chemical bond breaker ($/SF), edge foam isolation ($/LF), pour bed area required (with 10% note for access lanes). Panel schedule table: all types with SF/yd³/lbs/PSI/seq. Crane sizing engine: identifies heaviest panel, adds 1,500 lb rigging estimate, divides by 0.75 ASME B30.5 working load → minimum crane tonnage with OSHA ≥75%/≥90% lift plan requirements. Print ACI 551.1R-14 / TCA formal schedule: panel table with all columns, bond breaker material summary, crane sizing recommendation, ACI 551.1R min f'c 3,000 PSI before erection, EOR/Tilt-Up Contractor/GC Super triple sign-off. Copy tab-delimited report. Global localStorage. Most concrete contractors accept the structural engineer's panel schedule without pricing the pour bed setup: bond breaker, foam isolation, and crane sizing. Missing those items on a 50-panel building is a $15,000–$30,000 scope gap.
  - MIX DESIGN SUBMITTAL GENERATOR (📄): ACI 318-19 §26.4 / ACI 301-16 §4.2 — formal mix design approval package generator and tracking register. Add submittals: project, mix design ID/supplier ref, intended elements, design f'c PSI, cement type (10 presets: Type I/II/III + SCM blends), design slump (5 ranges), max w/c ratio, aggregate type (8 presets incl. El Paso caliche), max aggregate size, admixtures (12 ASTM types), supplier/plant, spec section reference, submitted date, notes. Auto-validation: w/c >0.55 triggers ACI 318-19 Table 19.3.3.1 exposure warning. Status cycle 8-stage: Draft → Submitted to GC → Under Architect Review → Under EOR Review → Approved → Approved with Conditions → Revise & Resubmit → Rejected. Dashboard: total, approved, pending, rejected. ⚠️ Red banner when any submittals pending approval — DO NOT PLACE per ACI 318-19 §26.4.3. Print formal submittal package: ACI 311.1/318-19 cited parameter table, 8-item required attachments checklist (trial batch records, sieve analysis, mill certs, SCM TDS, admixture TDS, batch plant calibration), contractor certification statement, 4-way Contractor/GC/Architect/EOR sign-off. Global localStorage. ACI 318-19 §26.4.3 requires approval at least 14 days before first pour — most concrete subs submit mix designs verbally or via email without a formal package, then have no record of approval date or approved parameters when batch tickets differ from what was approved.

- 09:55 UTC (Mar 26): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 3bf449f):
  - STRUCTURAL PROOF LOAD TEST LOG (🏋️): ACI 318-19 §27 / IBC §1708 in-place load test documentation. Logs element, design PSF, applied PSF, max deflection, span, residual deflection after unload, test engineer, reason for testing (9 presets). Auto-applies ACI 318-19 §27.4 pass criteria: max deflection ≤ L²/20,000h AND residual ≤ 25% of max deflection — PASS/FAIL verdict computed per test. Dashboard: total tests, passed (green), failed (red). Red ⛔ EOR structural evaluation banner when any tests fail per ACI 318-19 §27.6. Print formal log: failed rows highlighted red, structural stop notice, Engineer of Record/PM/Test Engineer triple sign-off. Global localStorage. Triggered when 28-day cylinder breaks fail (<85% f'c), suspected cold joints in structural members, or GC/owner disputes structural adequacy. Without a contemporaneous proof load record, there is no documented basis for accepting or rejecting the structure.
  - MATERIAL DELIVERY INSPECTION LOG (📦): ASTM A615/A706/A1064/A416/C94/C1064 incoming material verification at point of delivery. 18 material type presets. Per delivery: type, date, supplier, ticket/heat/cert#, quantity & unit, cert/COC status (4 options), received-by initials, issue flag (9 presets: None → Cert pending → Rejected), notes. Dashboard: deliveries, issues count, cert-pending count (red), rejected count. 🚫 HOLD banner when cert-pending items logged — DO NOT INSTALL per ACI 318-19 §26.3.2. Rejection tracking for returned materials. Print ASTM-referenced formal log: pending-cert items highlighted red, rejected items flagged, 5-year retention note, QC Inspector/PM/GC Special Inspector triple sign-off. Global localStorage (company-wide). GC inspectors and third-party QC routinely check cert files before approving rebar or concrete placement — without a delivery log, you have no record of when material arrived and whether certs were provided.
  - REPAIR COST BENCHMARKING LOG (🔬): ACI 546R-14 — institutional repair cost database built from actual completed jobs. 16 repair type presets each with El Paso 2025 market rate (crack routing $8.50/LF through rebar corrosion repair $110/SF). Per entry: type, project, date, qty, actual $/unit. Auto-computes total cost. Rate benchmark comparison table after any entries logged: your actual avg $/unit vs EP market rate with % variance — green ±10%, amber ±25%, red >±25%. Dashboard: jobs logged, repair types tracked, total cost. Entry log (last 20 jobs, newest first). Print formal ACI 546R-14 report: rate summary table (your actuals vs market with variance %), all job entries sorted by date, cost calibration guidance, Estimator/PM/Principal triple sign-off. Copy report. Global localStorage. Most concrete contractors have no historical repair cost data — they guess rates from memory or use 5-year-old numbers. After 10-20 jobs, this report identifies "we consistently underprice epoxy injection by 20%" and drives bid accuracy.

Override rate per item. OH% + profit% markup. Running cost total. Save named estimates to history. Print TxDOT Item 502 formal estimate with PM/GC dual sign-off. Copy plain-text. MUTCD Part 6 reference table (advance warning distances, sign sizes, flagger cert requirements, nighttime requirements). Traffic control is routinely missed or under-bid on parking lot and site concrete work adjacent to or in public ROW — El Paso has strict MUTCD enforcement and TxDOT ROW work requires PE-sealed MOT plans.

2. ✅ **TOOL 119 — Decorative / Stamped Concrete Estimator (🎨)** — ACI 302.1R / ASCC guidelines pricing calculator for specialty concrete finishes. 10 finish type presets with El Paso 2025 all-in rates: basic broom ($8.50/SF), salt finish ($10.50/SF), exposed aggregate ($12.50/SF), broom + integral color ($13/SF), stamped single color ($15/SF), stamped dual color ($19/SF), stencil/spray deck ($9/SF), epoxy flake broadcast ($13/SF), micro-topping polished ($16/SF), stamped overlay ($14/SF). Pattern complexity multiplier: simple ×1.0 → custom/high detail ×1.50. Color system add-on selector (no color → acid stain, +$0–$2/SF). Premium sealer add-on per SF. OH% + profit% markup. Live rate calculation showing all components. Large result cards showing total bid and all-in $/SF. Save estimates to history. Print formal ACI 302.1R/ASCC referenced estimate with color/pattern client approval block and Contractor/Client dual sign-off. ACI 302.1R curing notes (wax-based only — never sodium silicate on colored concrete), El Paso hot weather stamp window guidance (as short as 60 min), sealer reapplication recommendation (annual = maintenance contract opportunity). Most decorative concrete bids in El Paso are by feel — this puts a professional number to every decorative scope line item and forces client to approve color/pattern in writing before pour.

3. ✅ **TOOL 120 — Mid-Project QC Audit Checklist (📋)** — ACI 301-16 / ACI 318-19 / ACI 311.1 comprehensive 38-item structured QC audit across 7 categories: Mix Design & Materials (5 items: mix design approval, mill certs, batch tickets, cylinder records, hot/cold weather records), QC Testing (5 items: cylinder frequency, temp logging, slump testing, F-number survey, dimensional verification), Reinforcement (4 items: rebar placement, embedded items, bar chairs, PT tendons), Formwork (4 items: pre-pour inspection, release agent, stripping timing, OSHA shoring), Concrete Placement (5 items: cold joint plan, consolidation log, hot weather plan, cold weather protection, no unauthorized water addition), Curing (3 items: compound application log, minimum duration, evaporation retarder), Documentation (4 items: daily reports, records organization, special inspection notifications, ACI 214R statistics), Safety (4 items: silica controls, rebar impalement protection, tailgate log, OSHA 300 log), Contract & Compliance (4 items: unapproved mix design, pay app schedule, delay notices, TX lien notices). Per-item 5-status click cycle: OPEN → PASS → FAIL → PARTIAL → N/A. FAIL and PARTIAL items auto-surface a corrective action notes field. Live QC score (% computed from PASS + 0.5×PARTIAL / scored items). Color-coded score (green ≥90%, amber ≥75%, red <75%). ⛔ red alert banner when any FAIL items exist. Archive completed audits to history (15 max). Print ACI 311.1 formal audit report: FAIL rows highlighted red, PARTIAL rows amber, full category-grouped table, QC score at top, ACI 311.1 5-year retention language, QC Manager/PM/GC triple sign-off. This audit is designed to be run at project midpoint and before closeout — catches documentation gaps before the GC's final audit reveals them.

## Session: Mar 26, 2026 - 23:03 UTC
**Status:** Added 3 field operations & compliance tools (584 lines this session)

### New Features Added:
1. ✅ **TOOL 115 — Concrete Delivery Temperature Predictor (🌡️)** — ACI 305R Eq. 2-1 / ACI 306R §8 physics-based batch concrete temperature calculator. Inputs: 4 ingredient temperatures (mix water, cement, fine agg, coarse agg) with batch weights in cwt (hundredweights). 4 scenario presets (El Paso Summer 95°F → 110°F ambient requiring ice; El Paso Winter cold night; Standard Mild; Spring/Fall). Haul time + ambient temp → heat gain model (+0.15°F/min above 70°F). Outputs: predicted batch plant temp and estimated delivery temp. Verdicts: VIOLATION (>90°F), HIGH — USE ICE (>85°F), TOO COLD (<50°F), MARGINAL — HEAT, COMPLIANT. ACI 305R ice calculation: exact lb/yd³ needed to reach target 88°F. Save calc history. Print formal ACI 305R/306R log with QC/Batch Plant/PM triple sign-off. ACI 305R reference table (hot weather limits, cold weather minimums, retarder thresholds). El Paso summer: 110°F ambient + 95°F plant water + sun-heated aggregates = delivery at 97°F without ice — this computes exactly how much ice to call in before pour day.

2. ✅ **TOOL 116 — PEMB / Metal Building Foundation Embed Coordination Log (⚙️)** — ACI 318-19 §26.6 / AISC Design Guide 1 / MBMA Metal Building Systems Manual anchor bolt and embed plate tracking. 11 embed type presets (4/6/8-bolt anchor groups, moment frame plates, haunch angles, weld plates, purlin/rake/door embeds, mezzanine bases, custom). Per-item: column line/mark, bolt size/spec, bolt count, template/drawing reference, pour zone, grout requirement. 9-stage status cycle (Pending Drawing → Template Ordered → Template on Site → Rebar Caged → Inspected Pre-Pour → Concrete Placed → Survey Verification → Grout Pads Complete → GC Accepted) — click badge to advance. Dashboard: total embeds, accepted, pending, surveyed. Red ⛔ DO NOT ERECT banner when any items not GC-accepted; green all-clear when complete. AISC DG1 tolerance reference table (±1/4" horizontal per bolt, ±1" group offset, ±3/8" elevation, L/1000 plumb, 1.5"–2" grout clearance). Print ACI 318-19/AISC DG1 formal log: full embed schedule, AISC tolerance footnotes, 4-way sign-off (Field Inspector/Foreman/Structural Engineer/GC Super). Copy tab-delimited. Global localStorage. Anchor bolt errors are the #1 structural-steel erection delay on commercial PEMB jobs — wrong bolt = $3,500+/day crane sitting while drilling. This creates the pre-pour verification and survey record.

3. ✅ **TOOL 117 — Special Inspection Advance Notification Log (📋)** — ACI 301-16 §1.6 / IBC Chapter 17 / ACI 318-19 §26.13.3 — formal advance notice log for all IBC-required special inspection activities. 18 inspection type presets with auto-populated required notice hours (concrete placement 24hr, PT stressing 24hr, tilt-up erection 48hr, welding 8hr, compaction 8hr, etc.). Per-notice: inspection type, work/pour date, notification date, method (7 options: Email/Phone/Text/PM Software/RFI/Certified Mail/In Person), element/area, notified-by initials, required notice hours, notes/confirmation reference. Auto-computes actual lead time in hours vs required — red color when insufficient notice given. 6-stage status cycle: Sent-Pending → Confirmed → Inspector Declined-Reschedule → Inspector Present → Waived by GC → Complete-Records Filed. Red ⛔ banner when any inspections declined/reschedule required. Amber banner when pending confirmations exist. IBC Chapter 17 / ACI 318-19 reference table (10 inspection types with IBC code and notice requirements). Print ACI 301-16/IBC 17 formal log: work-date-sorted table with lead-time column color-coded green/red, declined rows highlighted, ACI 318-19 §26.13.3 legal language (24hr notice required; failure = rejection of work or required exposure at contractor cost; 7-year retention), triple sign-off. Copy tab-delimited. Project-scoped localStorage. GC can reject concrete work and require expensive exposure if pre-pour special inspection notification is not documented — this forces the habit on every pour.

3. ✅ **TOOL 123 — Contractor Self-Performance Capacity Planner (📊)** — Weekly capacity modeling to prevent over-commitment and identify crew/equipment shortfalls before mobilization. Capacity settings: yd³/crew-day productivity (default 35) and workdays/week (default 5). Crew roster: add named crews with worker count and status cycle (Available/Assigned/On Leave/Standby). Equipment list with status (Available/Deployed/In Maintenance/Rented Out/Not Owned). Jobs register (Active/Upcoming/Bidding/On Hold/Complete) with yd³, workdays, and start date — auto-computes crews needed per job at productivity rate. Capacity summary dashboard: available crews, assigned crews, crews needed for active jobs, max yd³/week at current availability. Crew shortfall alert (red) when active job needs exceed available crews — with specific recommendations (hire, subcontract, extend schedule, defer start). Capacity OK (green) when sufficient. Print formal capacity report: crew table, equipment table, jobs table with crews-needed column, summary stats, Operations/PM dual sign-off. Copy plain-text summary. Global localStorage. Most small concrete contractors have no formal capacity model — they commit to work and find out at mobilization they don't have enough finishers. This quantifies the shortfall 2–4 weeks in advance.

- 17:22 UTC (Mar 21): Proactive improvements shipped to takeoff.html (commit f2eb6e1):
  - BUGFIX: Waste/overage was double-counted — concrete was priced at adjustedYards AND a second wasteCost=subtotal*wastePct was added on top. Fixed: waste shows as concrete overage only (extra yards × price).
  - BUGFIX: Forms perimeter was estimated via sqrt(area)×4 (assumes square). Fixed: rectangles use exact 2*(w+h); polygons use actual edge-sum from stored points.
  - Added rebar spacing selector (12"/18"/24" OC each way) — visible only when bar rebar (#4 or #5) selected. LF/SF calculation now uses actual spacing.
  - Added page number badge (pg N) on each measurement item in sidebar list.
  - Added Scope Notes textarea in sidebar — persisted with project save/load, included in PDF export.
  All changes saved, committed, and pushed to GitHub (oncorai/OncorEstimate).
- 06:10 UTC (Mar 24): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 806e422):
  - BACKCHARGE NOTICE GENERATOR (⚡): Project-scoped log. 12 reason presets (rework, cleanup, damage, safety violation, etc). Per-entry: sub, date, amount, authorized-by, sub-notified flag. Status cycle: Open → Disputed → Agreed → Credited → Waived. Dashboard shows open count, open exposure $, credited total. Red alert banner when open items exist. Print formal Backcharge Notice: TX Property Code §53 rights language, 10-day written dispute window, PM/sub-ack/controller triple sign-off. Print All: full log table. Copy plain-text report. Without a contemporaneous backcharge notice, GCs pay subs and deny your claim. This creates the paper trail and forces written dispute or deduction.
  - REBAR MILL CERT TRACKER (🔩): Global localStorage. Per delivery: supplier, ticket #, bar size (#3-#11), ASTM grade (A615-Gr40/60/80, A706-Gr60 seismic, A1035-Gr100), LF received, heat number, cert ref, cert status. Auto-computes tons per delivery. Dashboard: total deliveries, certs OK count, pending count, total tons. Red alert banner when certs pending. Print formal ASTM A615/A706 compliance log: 10-column table, pending cert block highlighted, ACI 318-19 §26.3.2 note, QC/Field Super/GC Inspector triple sign-off. GC inspectors can reject bar installation if mill certs not on file — this creates the record before work starts.
  - SUB PERFORMANCE SCORECARD (⭐): Rate subs 1-5 on Schedule, Quality, Communication, Safety, Price Accuracy. Interactive star buttons. Aggregate summary: all-time averages ranked highest to lowest — Use Again (≥4.2)/Caution (≥3.0)/Do Not Use (<3.0). Detail log with per-criteria breakdown and notes. Print formal report: summary + detail tables, PM/Principal dual sign-off. Feeds Pre-Qual Registry. Most concrete contractors have no institutional sub memory — they rehire bad subs or forget great pump operators. This fixes both.
  Tool now at 31,533 lines.
- 05:01 UTC (Mar 24): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 364b921):
  - FORMWORK STRIPPING LOG (🏗️): Track when forms can be stripped per ACI 347R-14 minimum cure times. 6 element type presets (footings 12h, walls 24h, columns 24h, slab 168h/7d, beam soffit 336h/14d, elevated deck 336h). Cold weather adjustment per ACI 306R: avg temp <50°F doubles the minimum automatically. Live countdown: hours in / hours min / % cured. READY TO STRIP amber badge when unlock met. Mark Stripped stamps date. Print: ACI 347R stripping log with triple sign-off. 'When can I strip these wall forms?' is a daily question — now it has a documented ACI-cited answer instead of a gut call that can cause form blowouts or cylinder failures.
  - CONCRETE REPAIR WORK ORDER (🔧): ACI 301-16 §5.3 / ACI 546R-14 defect documentation. 14 defect type presets each with auto-populated ACI/ASTM repair method text (honeycombing → saw cut + non-shrink grout; spalling → saw cut perimeter + bonding agent + polymer concrete; structural crack → engineer notice + ASTM C881 epoxy injection; cold joint → probe test + rekeying; etc). Severity: Minor/Moderate/Severe/Critical (Critical = hold work). Status cycle: Open → In Progress → Closed. Auto-number RWO-001. Print: ACI 301/546R referenced table, QC/PM/GC triple sign-off. Without a RWO log, GCs claim you 'accepted' defects. This creates the paper trail.
  - SUBCONTRACTOR PO GENERATOR (📋): Generate formal Purchase Orders for pump, rebar, forming, saw cutting, testing subs (17 scope presets). Per PO: amount, payment terms (incl. pay-when-paid), retainage %, scope inclusions, exclusions. Auto-numbers PO-0001+. Print: company letterhead, contract sum / retainage held / net payable table, 8-clause T&C (COI requirements, TX lien waiver §53, change order authorization, 1-year warranty, TX venue), dual signature acceptance block. Verbal subcontracts and email-only arrangements are unenforceable when subs don't perform or file liens. This replaces that gap.
  Tool now at 30,792 lines.
- 03:53 UTC (Mar 24): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 10a8445):
  - SUBCONTRACTOR LIEN RELEASE TRACKER (📋): Per-draw lien release management keyed to active project. Add sub entries: company, scope (14 presets), draw number, draw amount, release type (Conditional Partial/Final, Unconditional Partial/Final), requested date, notes. "Mark Cleared" stamps date on release receipt. Dashboard: released count (green), pending count (red), total draw value. Red payment-hold alert banner when any required releases pending; green all-clear banner when all cleared. Print formal tracker: table with TX Property Code §53 legal language, payment-hold block, PM/AP/Principal triple sign-off. Copy plain-text report. Project-scoped (keys to project name). The most common way GCs lose money in disputes: paying a sub without getting a conditional lien release first. One uninsured rebar sub can put a mechanics lien on the owner's property — who then holds back YOUR payment. This tool forces the habit on every draw. TX Property Code §53 gives subs and suppliers lien rights automatically.
  - CONCRETE STRENGTH MATURITY CALCULATOR (🧪): ASTM C1074 Nurse-Saul method. Inputs: design f'c (PSI), avg ambient temp (°F), cement type (Type I/II or Type III), pour date/time. Computes 6 key milestones: form strip for walls (65%), light load (70%), PT stressing / shoring (75%), ACI 306R cold weather min (85%), reshoring / live load (90%), 28-day design strength (100%). Shows hours and days to each milestone. If pour date/time provided: shows estimated calendar date per milestone AND live current strength estimate (age-hours × dtF ÷ maturity curve → PSI and % progress bar, color-coded). ACI 305R hot weather alert >90°F; ACI 306R cold weather alert <40°F. Save settings. Print formal calc sheet: all parameters, milestones table, ASTM C1074 disclaimer, engineer/PM/GC inspector triple sign-off. localStorage. When can I strip these wall forms? When can I stress the PT? Is it safe to drive equipment on the slab? These are the questions that kill schedule or damage concrete if answered by gut instead of math. El Paso: summer 110°F accelerates early gain but depresses 28d strength; winter below-freezing nights can arrest maturity entirely.
  - OSHA 300 INCIDENT LOG (🦺): 29 CFR §1904 recordkeeping for all injury/illness events on company projects. Incident types: Near Miss, First Aid (non-recordable), and 4 OSHA Recordable categories (Medical Treatment / Restricted Work / Lost Time / Fatality). Per-event: employee, date, incident type, injury type (15 presets), body part (14 presets), project/location, days away, days restricted/transferred, description, physician/facility, witnesses. Dashboard: recordable count (red), near miss (amber), first aid (gray), days away (orange). Event log newest-first, color-coded border by severity. Delete per entry. Print OSHA Form 300: full table with recordable rows highlighted red, company summary totals, legal retention notice (5-year requirement), Feb 1–Apr 30 300A posting reminder, OSHA §1904.39 reporting deadlines (fatality 8h, hospitalization/amputation/eye 24h), Safety/PM/Principal triple sign-off. Copy plain-text log. Global localStorage (company-wide). Why: construction OSHA 300 logs are required with >10 employees; GC prequalification and OCIP programs review them. More practically, near-miss documentation drives safety culture — you can't fix what you don't track.
  Tool now at 30,183 lines.
- 02:47 UTC (Mar 24): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 24d412b):
  - WATER ADDITION LOG (💧): ASTM C94/C94M §11.8 compliance log for jobsite water addition events. Per-load: ticket #, truck ID, date/time, load yd³, gal added, ticket slump vs spec slump, authorized-by, engineer notified flag, notes. Real-time violation check — ASTM C94 allows max 1 gal/yd³; exceeding it invalidates w/c ratio compliance. Color-coded per-load status (✓ ASTM C94 OK / ⚠️ VIOLATION with exact overage). Dashboard: total loads, violations count (red badge), total gal added. Formal print: ASTM C94 §11.8 full compliance log with violation alert block, QC/Foreman/PM triple sign-off, ASTM legal language (90-min/300-rev discharge rule). Copy plain-text log. Global localStorage. Water addition disputes are extremely common — El Paso summer pours, GCs adding water after placement, drivers adding water before testing. Without a contemporaneous log with authorization trail, you have $0 in a dispute.
  - SUB PRE-QUALIFICATION REGISTRY (⭐): Formally evaluate and approve subcontractors and vendors before awarding scope. Per-sub: company, trade/scope (13 presets), contact/phone, years in business, bonding capacity ($), CGL coverage, WC status, TX license/reg #, rating (1-5), past projects/references, notes. Status cycle: Pending Review → Approved → Conditional → Rejected → On Hold (click badge to advance). Summary: total subs, approved count, pending count. Print formal registry: 10-column table, dual PM/Principal sign-off, qualification disclaimer. Copy plain-text list. Global localStorage. As JFS grows into a GC role, formal sub preQual is required for surety bond programs (typically required above $500k bonding). Also protects against uninsured subs on your jobs — a WC gap from a sub means the GC's policy picks up the claim.
  - DAILY COST TRACKER (📅): Running actual vs. budget cost log by day. Budget setup: project name + budget total with "↙ From Bid" to pull current bid amount. Per-entry: date, category (8 options: Concrete Materials/Rebar/Forms/Labor/Pump Rental/Equipment/Subcontractors/Misc), description, amount, notes/invoice ref. Dashboard: total spent, remaining (red if over budget), avg daily burn rate, estimated days remaining at current burn rate, % of budget progress bar (color-codes red at 90%). Category breakdown chart (bar %) shows where money is going. Recent entries list (last 15, newest first). Print formal cost report: budget summary table, category breakdown, all entries table with totals, PM/Accounting/Principal triple sign-off. Copy report. Per-project localStorage (keyed to project name). Answers "where am I vs budget right now?" and "how fast am I burning?" daily without waiting for accounting.
  Tool now at 29,568 lines.
- 01:40 UTC (Mar 24): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 15fa612):
  - PRE-POUR APPROVAL CHECKLIST (✅): ACI 305R / ACI 306R / OSHA 1926 — 30-item structured sign-off across 6 categories (Weather, Mix & Materials, Site Prep, Equipment, QC & Inspection, Curing Plan). Click-to-cycle each item: OK → RISK → N/A → Open. Left-border color codes hot-weather items (orange, ACI 305R) vs cold-weather items (blue, ACI 306R). Risk items surface in a red alert block at top. "CLEARED FOR POUR" green banner at 100% all-clear. Project/element/pour date/foreman header. Print formal sign-off sheet: table with ACI refs, 3-column initials column, RISK items block, Foreman/QC Inspector/GC Superintendent triple signature, ACI hot/cold threshold notes at bottom. Copy plain-text checklist. localStorage per tool. Example: ACI 305R evaporation rate > 0.20 lb/ft²/hr on a dry El Paso summer day with 15+ mph wind → pump brake failure/plastic shrinkage → this checklist forces the weather check before calling "start pour."
  - EQUIPMENT DOWNTIME LOG (🔧): Contemporaneous record of equipment breakdown events for delay claims and rental company credit recovery. Per-event: equipment type (13 presets: boom pump, line pump, laser screed, ride-on trowel, etc.), project, date, cause (9 types: mechanical/hydraulic/electrical/fuel/operator error/third-party/weather/rental non-delivery/other), downtime hours, repair cost, lost production yd³, installed $/yd³ rate, resolution description, notes, rental claim flag. Auto-computes lost revenue (lost yd³ × $/yd³). Dashboard: event count, total down hours, total repair cost, combined impact $. Event log (newest first) with delete. Print formal Downtime Report: 10-column table with totals, Equipment Manager/PM/Controller triple sign-off, legal note on delay claim use. Copy plain-text report. Global localStorage (company-wide). Example: boom pump mechanical failure = 4 hrs down × 40 yd³/hr × $180/yd³ installed = $28,800 lost revenue + $2,400 repair call = $31,200 impact. Without a contemporaneous log, you'll get $0 on a T&M claim.
  - SUBMITTAL TRANSMITTAL GENERATOR (📤): Professional transmittal letter generator for Division 03 submittals — mix designs, shop drawings, material certs. 13 submittal type presets. Action types: For Approval / For Information / For Record / Revise and Resubmit / As Built / For Review. Fields: transmittal no., date, to/from parties, project, GC/owner, contract no., spec section, copies, description, resubmittal flag, remarks. "↙ From Company Settings" and active project pre-fill. "Generate Transmittal Letter" produces full printable: company letterhead, header with TX no./date, project/party tables, submittal details with colored action badge, 14-day response notice, action-specific warnings (no work until approval; resubmittal acknowledgment), dual signature block (Submitted By / Received By). "Copy Email Draft" generates ready-to-send email with full subject line and structured body — paste directly into Gmail/Outlook. localStorage per tool. GCs increasingly require formal transmittals for all Division 03 submittals — sending them in email without a transmittal is unprofessional and creates no audit trail for approval dates.
  Tool now at 28,627 lines.
- 00:34 UTC (Mar 24): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 26f05a6):
  - CERTIFIED PAYROLL GENERATOR (📜): WH-347-format certified payroll for Davis-Bacon / prevailing wage work. Pairs with Prevailing Wage Tracker. Project + GC + contract header. Per-employee: name, SSN last 4, classification (8 roles: Laborer → Superintendent), reg hours, OT hours, base $/hr. Auto-computes gross wages (OT at 1.5×), estimated FICA (7.65%), estimated FIT (12% flat), other deductions, net pay. Summary strip: employee count, total hours, total gross, est. FICA. "Print WH-347" generates full DOL-format printable with certification statement (29 CFR §3.3), legal penalty notice (18 U.S.C. §1001, up to $1k + 1yr imprisonment for falsification), triple signature block. Copy plain-text payroll report. Global localStorage. Many public/municipal/TxDOT projects require certified payroll weekly submissions — now takes 5 minutes instead of 30+.
  - PROJECT RISK MATRIX (⚠️): Probability × impact risk register for pre-bid risk assessment. 8 default concrete risks pre-loaded with real probabilities and impact estimates (slump OOS, escalation, rain delay, rebar re-submittal, pump failure, scope creep, cylinder failure, labor shortage). Add/edit/delete risks. Per-risk: description, probability %, impact $, category (11 options), mitigation notes. Auto-computes EV per risk. Sorts by EV descending (highest exposure first). Summary: total EV, high-EV count, contingency gap. "↙ From Bid" pulls contingency amount from active bid. ✅ COVERED / ⚠️ UNDEREXPOSED verdict with exact dollar gap. "↺ Defaults" reloads 8 default risks. Print formal risk register with 3-way sign-off (Estimator/PM/Principal). Copy plain-text report. Global localStorage. Example: 30% probability of soft subgrade at $15k impact = $4,500 EV. Knowing your contingency is $12,000 but EV is $28,000 means you're $16k underexposed before bidding.
  - NONCONFORMING CONCRETE REJECTION NOTICE (🚫): ACI 318-19 §26.12.4 / ACI 301-16 §1.6.8 formal rejection documentation. 10 nonconformance type presets (slump OOS, temp >95°F, temp <50°F, ASTM C94 time/rev limit, added water, wrong mix, air OOS, 28-day failure, 7-day projection failure, other). Per-event: project, supplier, ticket #, truck ID, load yd³, date/time, rejection type, specified vs actual value, description, disposition (5 options: reject/return, reject/dump, accept with conditions, hold for test, remediate), noticed-by, inspector present, engineer notified, engineer name, remediation notes. "Log This Rejection Event" saves to timestamped log. Full rejection event log with reverse-chronological view, delete per entry. "Print Rejection Notice" generates ACI 318/ASTM C94 referenced printable log with legal citation block, QC triple signature block, 24h engineer notification reminder. Copy plain-text log. Global localStorage (company-wide across all projects). The silent killer on disputed concrete work: GC claims you accepted a bad load because you have no contemporaneous written rejection. This forces the habit.
  Tool now at 27,735 lines.
- 23:28 UTC (Mar 23): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 803bc9f):
  - JOINT SEALANT ESTIMATOR (🔩): Calculates control joint LF from slab SF and ACI 360R-10 spacing rules. Expansion joint LF manual input. Per ACI 360R §9 / ASTM C920 reservoir method: computes sealant volume (width × depth), 29 fl oz tube count with 10% waste. 5 sealant types with preset $/LF: polyurethane ($4.20), silicone ($5.80), epoxy ($7.50), hot-pour ($2.40), acrylic latex ($1.80). Labor $/LF separate. Optional backer rod (+$0.18/LF) and primer (+$0.22/LF). Results: CJ LF, EJ LF, total LF, tube count, itemized costs, total scope, $/LF all-in. Copy + printable estimate with sign-off and ACI 1193/C920 disclaimer. Global localStorage. Joint sealant is consistently missed or under-bid on warehouse slabs — a 50k SF slab at 12' spacing has ~8,300 LF of CJ at $5/LF = $41k in scope.
  - ACI CYLINDER TESTING BUDGET (🧫): Per ACI 301-16 §1.6.8 — 1 set per 50 yd³ OR each placement day, whichever requires MORE sets, minimum 1 set per element. Add pours manually or "↙ From Takeoff" to import all measurements. Each pour: element name, yd³, pour date. Auto-calculates sets required, cylinder count, 7-day and 28-day break dates. Dashboard: total sets, total cylinders, testing cost (sets × $/set), misc lab costs, total QC budget. Copy + printable testing schedule with 7/28-day break date columns and PM sign-off. ACI 301 failure threshold: 28-day break < 85% specified f'c on 2-cylinder average. Typical El Paso labs charge $30–50/break. Missing QC budget on a 500 yd³ pour (10 sets × 4 cyls × $45/break = $1,800) is a soft cost estimators routinely miss.
  - COI REQUEST GENERATOR (📄): Generate a professional Certificate of Insurance request letter for any subcontractor or vendor. Fields: sub name/contact, project name/address, GC/owner, COI due date. Coverage limits: CGL each occurrence, general aggregate, auto CSL, umbrella, WC E/L each accident, cancellation notice days. Endorsements: Additional Insured (ongoing + completed ops), Waiver of Subrogation, Primary & Non-Contributory — each toggle-able. Certificate holder name + address. "↙ From Settings" and "↙ From Project" pre-fill buttons. "Generate COI Request Letter" produces full formal letter with all coverage tables, endorsement list, cancellation notice, and TX WC disclaimer. Preview inline. Copy + Print. Global localStorage. GCs demand COIs before mobilization — having a generator means you can request subs' coverage in 60 seconds instead of writing it from scratch.

Line-by-line breakdown with total loaded $/hr and burden % (color: green ≤35%, amber ≤45%, red >45%). Annual and daily cost shown. - 20:19 UTC (Mar 25): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 8f0d524):
  - SLIPFORM / CONTINUOUS POUR CALCULATOR (🏗️): ACI 347R-14 / ACI 347.1R compliant calculator for vertical slipform operations (silos, chimneys, core walls, retaining walls, cooling towers). Geometry engine for circular, rectangular, and linear elements with automatic cross-section and perimeter calculation. Inputs: height, diameter/length, wall thickness, slip rate (in/hr), form depth, initial set time, concrete PSI, labor/equipment costs. Outputs: total yd³, pour duration (continuous hours), placement rate (yd³/hr), truck delivery interval. Critical ACI 347.1R safety check: time-in-form vs initial set time — if slip rate exceeds form-exit strength capability, red alert with max safe rate recommendation. Production schedule (25/50/75/100% milestones). Cost breakdown: concrete + labor + equipment rental. Save calculations to history. Print ACI 347R/347.1R formal calc sheet with geometry table, production summary, cost estimate, and triple sign-off. Slipform is rare but high-value specialty work (silos, grain elevators, tall core walls) — having a calculator means JFS can bid these jobs professionally instead of declining scope he doesn't understand.
  - SUBSTANTIAL COMPLETION WORKFLOW (✅): AIA A201 §9.8 project closeout process tracker. 25-item checklist across 5 categories (Work Completion, Testing & QC, Documentation, Inspections, Financial). Click-to-toggle completion status per item with progress bar. Project status cycle: Not Started → In Progress → Ready for Review → GC Accepted → Owner Accepted → Disputed. Fields: SC date, Final Completion date, GC rep, Owner rep, notes/punch list summary. Print formal Certificate of Substantial Completion (AIA G704 format): project info, certification statement, outstanding items, warranties & responsibilities section, 4-party signature block (Contractor/GC/Architect/Owner), occupancy notice. Project-scoped localStorage. Substantial Completion is THE milestone that triggers warranty start, risk shift, and retainage release timeline — having a structured workflow prevents missed items and produces the formal certificate GCs and owners require.
  - ESTIMATING ACCURACY ANALYSIS (📊): Post-job variance tracking to calibrate future bids. Log completed jobs with bid vs actual costs across 10 categories (Concrete Material, Rebar/Mesh, Formwork, Labor, Pump/Equipment, Subcontractors, Testing/QC, Overhead, Contingency Used, Other). Per-job summary: total bid, total actual, variance %, lessons learned notes. Aggregate dashboard: jobs tracked, total bid vs actual, average variance %, overrun/underrun counts. Category breakdown table: total bid/actual per category, variance $ and %, automatic insight text ("Consistently underestimate — increase bid factor" / "Consistently overestimate — tighten pricing" / "Within normal range"). Print formal Estimating Accuracy Report: category variance table with recommendations, job-by-job summary, usage guidance. Global localStorage. The purpose: after 10-20 jobs, this data shows "we underestimate labor by 8%" or "our rebar numbers are within 2%" — and you adjust bid factors accordingly. Most small contractors never close this loop; they guess markup without data.
  Tool now at 49,258 lines.
- 19:11 UTC (Mar 25): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit 0d16cf4):
  - SHOTCRETE / GUNITE ESTIMATOR (🔫): ACI 506R compliant material quantity and cost calculator. Dry-Mix vs Wet-Mix method selector. Application direction selector (vertical/overhead/slope/horizontal). Auto-computes ACI 506R rebound waste % by method+direction (dry overhead = 30%, dry vert = 20%, wet = 5–10%). Calculates placed volume, rebound waste yd³, and ORDER quantity (place/(1−rebound)). Production rate estimate (shifts + hours per ACI 506R nozzleman benchmarks). Full cost breakdown: material, labor (nozzleman + crew), equipment/mob, OH+profit. $/SF and $/yd³ all-in results. ACI 506R compliance notes: no rebound reuse (§5.4.3), nozzleman certification requirement. Save/delete estimate history. Print formal estimate with ACI references and client acceptance block. El Paso: pools, retaining walls, slope stabilization, canal lining. Shotcrete estimators consistently under-order for overhead applications — 30% rebound means 43% more material than placed volume.
  - ELEVATED DECK SHORING REMOVAL LOG (🏗️): ACI 347R-14 Table 6.1 shore removal strength tracking. Log pour events: element/floor, pour date, f'c PSI, max span, element type (one-way/two-way slab/PT/beam/composite/elevated SOG), reshoring requirement. Auto-computes ACI 347R minimum strength % before shore removal (70% for slabs <10ft span, 85% for 10-20ft, 100% for >20ft; 85/100% for beams). Shows minimum PSI required (% × design f'c). Status cycle: Shores In Place → Cyl Break 7d Pending → Cyl Break 28d Pending → Strength Met — Awaiting EOR Authorization → Shore Removal Authorized → Shores Removed. Dashboard: total floors, open holds count. Red alert banner listing all floors with shores still in place. Stamps removal date when complete. Print ACI 347R-14 formal log: full table with min PSI column, open-hold alert block, EOR authorization triple sign-off. Global localStorage. Most concrete GCs remove shores on a time schedule rather than strength — wrong method, and wrong on projects with heavier-than-normal construction loads above.
  - POST-POUR CORE DRILL SCHEDULE (🔵): ACI 318-19 §6.3 post-pour coring coordination and tracking. Auto-numbers CD-001+. 12 purpose presets (plumbing sleeve, conduit, drain, anchor bolt, structural tie-in, core sample, inspection port, HVAC, etc.). Per-entry: element, diameter, plan location/grid, requested by, driller company, scheduled date, PT slab flag (GPR scan required), fire-rated assembly flag (IBC §714 fire stop required), EOR approval flag. Status cycle: Pending Schedule → Scheduled → Drilled–Inspection Pending → Complete → On Hold–EOR Approval → Cancelled. EOR-flagged entries auto-set to On Hold. Dashboard: total, pending, EOR hold, complete. Red ⛔ banner when EOR-hold cores exist. Conflict detection engine: retarder+accelerator (direct chemical conflict), WR+HRWR (redundant), corrosion inhibitor (calcium nitrite is accelerating)+retarder. OSHA 1926.703(a)(1): formwork shall be inspected before and during concrete placement — this creates the contemporaneous paper trail that proves the inspection occurred and clears it before pour.

  - VERTICAL SURFACE REPAIR METHOD SELECTOR (🏗️): ACI 546R-14 / ICRI 310.2R / ACI 301-16 §5.3 — ACI-cited repair method database for 8 defect categories. Honeycombing shallow: PMM (ACI 546R §4.2) vs Non-Shrink Grout Pack (ASTM C1107). Honeycombing structural: Epoxy Injection (ASTM C881) vs Form-and-Pump (full EOR scope). Spalling surface: PMM Overlay (ICRI 310.2R, CSP 3-5) vs High-Build Epoxy Mortar (ASTM C881 Type VI). Spalling structural: Full Depth Replacement + Dowels (ACI 318-19) vs Shotcrete (ACI 506R). Crack non-structural: Routing & Sealing (ASTM C920) vs Rigid Epoxy Fill (ASTM C881 Type I). Crack structural: Pressure Injection (ASTM C881) vs Stitching with Anchors (ACI 546R §7). Tie holes: Dry-Pack Mortar vs Precast Plug. Bug holes: Rubbing Compound Skin Coat vs Epoxy Paste Fill. Each option: procedure steps, spec reference, El Paso 2025 cost range, product recommendations. - 09:12 UTC (Mar 27): 3 proactive improvements shipped (commit c176205):
  - CONCRETE DELIVERY REJECTION LOG & LETTER GENERATOR (🚫): ASTM C94/C94M / ACI 301-16 formal rejection documentation. 12 rejection reason presets with ASTM/ACI citations (slump high/low, temp >95°F/<50°F, 90-min time exceeded, 300 rev exceeded, unauthorized water, wrong mix/PSI, air OOS, contaminated). Per rejection: project, supplier, date/time, ticket #, truck, yd³, spec vs actual values, disposition (Returned/Dumped/Accepted w/ Conditions). Dashboard: total rejections, returned count, total yd³ rejected. Print formal rejection letter per load with ASTM C94 §11 purchaser rights citation and triple sign-off (Contractor/Driver/Plant). Print full log. Most concrete disputes with ready-mix plants happen weeks after delivery when there's no contemporaneous record. ASTM C94 §11 gives contractors the right to reject non-conforming concrete at delivery — but only with documentation.
  - READY-MIX PLANT BACKUP CONTACT CARD (📞): Emergency backup plant contacts for pour day. Per plant: name, city, dispatch phone, sales rep + cell, emergency line, 3000/4000/5000 PSI pricing, lead time (hrs), rank (Primary/Backup/Emergency), notes (has ice, 24hr dispatch, etc.). Directory sorted by rank with color-coded badges. Clickable phone links for instant dialing. Print formal contact card for truck cab and job trailer. When primary plant has equipment failure, cement shortage, or can't meet delivery schedule, having 2-3 backup contacts with pricing prevents total project shutdown. Most contractors scramble on pour day when their plant fails — this creates the pre-built emergency list.
  - CONCRETE PLACEMENT HOLD POINT REGISTER (⛔): ACI 301-16 / IBC Chapter 17 inspection hold point tracking. 11 hold point type presets (Pre-Placement Rebar, Formwork, Embedded Items, PT Tendons; First Delivery Acceptance; Placement Observation; Form Stripping Auth; PT Stressing; Special Inspection; EOR Verification; GC/Owner Acceptance). Per hold point: project, element/area, pour date, type, inspector name + phone, status. 6-status cycle: Pending → Scheduled → Notified → Released → Waived → Failed. Red FAILED banner when any hold points failed (work stopped until resolved). Amber PENDING banner when hold points unreleased. Green ALL RELEASED banner when clear. Dashboard: total, pending, released, failed. Print formal register with IBC 1704.2.3 citation. IBC 1704.2.3: "Work shall not proceed beyond a designated inspection point until the special inspector has inspected and approved the work." Pouring without required inspection = potential rejection of work and structural re-evaluation at contractor expense.
